2. June 2026 + 6 = June 2032

*Remember:* Customer account records: 6 years after the account closes.

</details>

**Q8060** (§15.4.7 · FINRA section 4) — Which of the following records must be preserved for three years?
I. Order tickets
II. Blotters
III. Trade confirmations
IV. Customer complaints

- A. I and III
- B. II and IV
- C. I, II and III
- D. II, III and IV

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A.** Order tickets and trade confirmations are kept for 3 years. Blotters are kept for 6 years and complaints for 4.

*Remember:* 3 years: order tickets, confirms, communications; 4 years: complaints; 6 years: blotters and ledgers.

</details>

**Q9056** (§15.1 · FINRA section 1) — A broker-dealer plans to begin offering commodity futures contracts to its customers. Which federal agency regulates the futures markets, and which organization is the futures industry's self-regulatory organization?

- A. SEC; FINRA
- B. CFTC; NFA
- C. Federal Reserve; CME Group
- D. CFTC; FINRA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B.** The Commodity Futures Trading Commission regulates commodities and futures markets, and the National Futures Association is the futures industry's SRO.

*Remember:* Futures: CFTC regulates; NFA is the SRO (Series 3).

</details>

**Q9065** (§15.2.2 · FINRA section 3) — A registered rep introduces several customers to a friend's private startup and arranges for them to buy shares directly from the company. She will receive no compensation. Under FINRA Rule 3280, she must:

- A. Give her firm prior written notice describing the transaction and her role
- B. Do nothing, because the rule applies only to compensated transactions
- C. Obtain the firm's written approval and have the sales recorded on its books
- D. Notify the firm in writing within 30 days after the sales are completed

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A.** Every private securities transaction requires prior written notice to the firm. When the rep receives no compensation, the firm acknowledges the notice and may impose conditions; approval and supervision on its books are required only when she is compensated.

*Remember:* Private securities transaction: prior written notice always; written approval + supervision if compensated.

</details>

**Q9066** (§15.2.1 · FINRA section 3) — On the day XYZ options expire, a trader holding a large position in XYZ calls enters a burst of buy orders in the last few minutes before 4:00 pm to push XYZ's closing price above the strike. This is:

- A. Front-running
- B. Capping
- C. Marking the close
- D. Wash trading

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C.** Trading near the end of the session to manipulate the closing price, which sets option values, margin and fund NAVs, is marking the close.

*Remember:* Marking the close = trading near the close to manipulate the closing price.

</details>

**Q9067** (§15.2.3 · FINRA section 3) — Which of the following is NOT insider trading?

- A. A banker's spouse buys a target's stock after overhearing a confidential deal call at home
- B. A company engineer sells his shares before a failed product test is announced
- C. An investor buys after a director's friend tells her about an unannounced takeover bid
- D. An analyst buys after piecing together public filings and store visits, none material alone

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D.** Building a conclusion from public information and non-material private details is the mosaic theory and is legal. The other choices all involve trading on material, non-public information.

*Remember:* Mosaic theory: combining public and non-material information is legal; trading on MNPI is not.

</details>

**Q9068** (§15.4.6 · FINRA section 3) — A rep recommends that a 58-year-old retail customer roll her 401(k) balance into an IRA at the rep's firm. Which statement is correct under Regulation Best Interest?

- A. Reg BI applies, because recommending a rollover or account type is covered
- B. Reg BI does not apply, because no specific security was recommended
- C. Only Department of Labor rules apply to rollovers, not Reg BI
- D. Reg BI applies only if she later buys a security the rep recommends

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A.** Reg BI covers recommendations of securities, investment strategies and account types, which expressly includes rollovers, made to retail customers.

*Remember:* Reg BI covers recommendations of securities, strategies and account types (including rollovers) to retail customers.

</details>

**Q9069** (§15.4.3 · FINRA section 4) — Through a firm-approved and supervised account, a registered rep replies in real time to a follower's question during an interactive social media chat. How is the post treated under FINRA rules?

- A. As a retail communication needing prior principal approval
- B. Like correspondence: supervised and retained, with no prior principal approval
- C. As an institutional communication exempt from review
- D. As a public appearance with no retention requirement

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B.** Interactive, real-time social media content is treated like correspondence: it must be supervised and retained but needs no principal pre-approval. Static content such as a profile page is a retail communication that needs approval.

*Remember:* Social media: static content = retail (pre-approval); interactive posts = treated like correspondence (supervise, retain).

</details>

**Q9070** (§15.4.3 · FINRA section 4) — A broker-dealer keeps its records of retail communications. Along with a copy of each communication, the record must show:

- A. The names of every customer who received it
- B. FINRA's written approval of the piece
- C. The commissions earned from sales it generated
- D. The approving principal's name and the dates of first and last use

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D.** Communication records are kept for 3 years and must include the name of the principal who approved the piece and the dates it was first and last used.

*Remember:* Communications records: 3 years, with approving principal's name and dates of first/last use.

</details>

**Q9071** (§15.4.5 · FINRA section 4) — A registered rep is terminated by her firm on May 1. Which statement about Form U5 is correct?

- A. The rep must file it on CRD within 30 days
- B. The rep must sign it before the firm can file it
- C. The firm must file it within 30 days and give the rep a copy
- D. The firm must file it within 10 business days, with no copy to the rep

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C.** The firm files Form U5 within 30 days of termination, must give the departing rep a copy, and must be truthful. The rep cannot be forced to sign it.

*Remember:* Form U5: firm files within 30 days of termination, gives the rep a copy.

</details>

**Q9072** (§15.4.5 · FINRA section 4) — Which of the following activities may an unregistered sales assistant perform?
I. Accepting a customer's order when the rep is out
II. Processing new account paperwork
III. Calling prospects to find out whether they have money to invest
IV. Mailing firm-approved forms that customers asked for

- A. I and II
- B. I and III
- C. III and IV
- D. II and IV

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D.** Unregistered persons may do clerical and ministerial work. They may not take customer orders or qualify prospects.

*Remember:* Unregistered staff: clerical only; no orders, no qualifying prospects, no discussing investments.

</details>

**Q9073** (§15.4.5 · FINRA section 4) — Which of the following would subject an applicant to statutory disqualification?
I. A misdemeanor forgery conviction 6 years ago
II. A felony DUI conviction 3 years ago
III. A misdemeanor reckless-driving conviction last year
IV. A felony conviction 14 years ago

- A. I and II
- B. I and IV
- C. II and III
- D. I, II and IV

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A.** Statutory disqualification covers any felony and any securities- or money-related misdemeanor (such as forgery) within the past 10 years. A traffic misdemeanor doesn't count, and a felony 14 years ago is outside the 10-year window.

*Remember:* Statutory disqualification: any felony or financial misdemeanor (fraud, theft, forgery) within 10 years.

</details>

**Q9074** (§15.4.7 · FINRA section 4) — A registered rep's employment with a broker-dealer ends in August 2026. Until when must the firm keep her Form U4, Form U5 and employment application?

- A. August 2028
- B. August 2032
- C. August 2029
- D. August 2030

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C.** Employment applications and Forms U4/U5 of terminated persons are kept for 3 years after termination: August 2026 + 3 years = August 2029.

1. Retention = 3 years after termination
2. August 2026 + 3 years = August 2029

*Remember:* U4/U5 and employment records of terminated persons: 3 years after termination.

</details>
